- 2026-10-09 — bundle 9 turn 17: the 320px location-row label expectation reads its `sr-only` class instead of Playwright visibility (a 1 px `sr-only` box counts as visible, so `toBeHidden()` could never pass); the product and the test's other assertions are unchanged.

No migration.

Workflow refrozen (`ci.yml` reverted verbatim to 2ffbaa8) and the hardcoded-string guard now self-tests IN-SCRIPT against its fixture pair (bad ≥1, good 0; mismatch exits 2 with SELF-TEST FAILED). DECLARED IDENTITY LAW: tests that mutate their own auth state carry the Playwright tag `@private-identity` (IMP-1/2/3, RP-6, MF-1/2/5/6/7); `useJobSuperAdmin` reads `test.info().tags` and hands a tagged test a private mint (`mintPrivateSuperAdmin`, the pre-L4 path) instead of the pool. - 2026-09-07 — IE-2: ATTRIBUTE IMPORT — PREVIEW → CONFIRM/DISCARD → UNDO. Migration `20260907185010_5736cb8a` (mark 20260907200000) registers `categories:import` (step-up required, granted to `super_admin` only), creates the RPC-only capture table `attribute_import_revisions` (deny-all RLS, `GRANT ALL TO service_role`, batch index) and lands three gated doors over one shared planner: `attr_import_plan` (internal, no client EXECUTE — read back in-file), `admin_preview_attribute_import` (writes nothing), `admin_commit_attribute_import` (F5 order: permission → step-up → per-user advisory lock → capture old→new, batch-tagged → mutate; `change=none` writes nothing, so a round trip is idempotent) and `admin_undo_attribute_import` (restores the batch's untouched rows, audited). Refusals are PER ROW with a reason: missingKey, duplicateKey, badAction, unknownType, malformedOptions, badParent (DEC-045 — an option `parent` must exist on the `depends_on` definition's list), blastRadius, unknownCategory, unknownAttribute, outOfScope, inheritedRow (edit at the origin), badCardRank, formula. New server route `src/routes/api/admin/attributes/import.ts` (POST `mode=preview|commit|undo`) never trusts the client's parse: bearer → caller-context publishable client → server-side RFC-4180/BOM parse, 1 MB and 5 000-row caps, strict IE-1 headers plus an optional trailing `action` column (upsert · unlink · delete — deletion is never inferred from absence), a raw `= + - @` cell refused per row while the export's `'`-neutralised cell round-trips, and a SHA-256 digest over both files that the commit must match (409 `fileChanged`); every failure logs `[ssr-error] /api/admin/attributes/import …` and relays 401/403/409/413/428. UI: an "Import attributes" control beside Export, gated on `categories:import`, opening `ImportAttributesDialog` — file pick → preview counts and refusals → Confirm (through the page's step-up guard) or Discard, then "Undo last import" for that batch; an active category filter travels as the scope, so rows outside the subtree are refused in the preview. Keys EN+AM under `admin.attributes.import.*` plus `admin.roles.perm.action.import`. E2E: AT-20 round-trip no-op, AT-21 change → commit → undo (DB truth), AT-22 bad header / raw formula / unknown slug with nothing written, AT-23 no control and 403 for a `categories:view`-only scratch role (401 with no bearer), AT-24 edited bytes refused against the preview digest, AT-25 an invalid option `parent` refused.
- 2026-09-07 — IE-2b: IDEMPOTENT ROUND-TRIP (INC-177). Corrective migration (mark 20260907210000) re-declares `attr_import_plan` and adds two pure normalisers, `attr_option_norm(jsonb)` and `attr_json_or_null(text)` (in-file REVOKEs, DEC-022-B). ROOT CAUSE: every ratified definition stores its options as OBJECTS, IE-1 serialises them with `jsonb_array_elements_text` joined by `|` (so each segment is JSON text), and the planner split that back into plain STRINGS — so re-importing an untouched export reported every definition as "changed". The planner now parses a pipe segment beginning with `{` as JSON (a segment that starts like an object but does not parse is `malformedOptions`), and compares options SEMANTICALLY: `attr_option_norm` folds both sides to an ordered list of `{value, label_en, label_am, parent}` with null ≡ absent ≡ `""`, every field `btrim`-ed, the stored order preserved, a `{"depends_on": …}` meta entry excluded, and a bare string option read as value-only. `label_en` is compared through the same trim-and-default expression the write uses (blank falls back to the key), booleans are parsed from true/t/1/yes, card_rank empty ≡ null, and a DEC-045 `parent` is only validated when it is non-empty. IDEMPOTENCY PROOF IN THE MIGRATION: a DO block builds the whole current library in its own export shape (definitions with pipe-joined option text, own links with `origin = category_slug`), previews it through `attr_import_plan` and RAISEs unless adds = changes = refusals = 0. E2E: AT-20 becomes the invariant — the whole library is exported through the real route and re-imported through the DIALOG's file picker (`setInputFiles`), the preview must read 0 added · 0 changed · 0 unlinked · 0 deleted · 0 refused with `unchanged` = every data row of both files, and Discard leaves no `attribute_import_revisions` row (DB truth); AT-26 proves option key order and an explicit `"label_am": null` against an absent field preview unchanged.
- 2026-09-07 — IE-2b rider: AT-20 is now concurrency-proof. A sibling spec may mint or destroy its own scratch fixtures between the export and the preview, which read as a phantom add; the invariant therefore drops every exported record naming an `e2e_attr_` attribute or an `e2e-cat-` category (quote-aware record split, BOM + CRLF preserved) and asserts `unchanged` against the STABLE library rows alone. Local run: 52 passed (desktop-1280 + mobile-360).
- 2026-09-07 — CAT-IE: CATEGORY EXPORT + IMPORT (preview → confirm/discard → undo) THROUGH THE LIFECYCLE DOORS. Migration mark 20260907230000 creates the RPC-only capture table `category_import_revisions` (deny-all RLS, `GRANT ALL TO service_role`), the semantic normalisers and subtree helpers, and four gated doors: `admin_export_categories(text)` (`categories:view`, scope = a slug's subtree), `admin_preview_category_import` (writes nothing), `admin_commit_category_import` (F5 order: `categories:import` → step-up → per-user advisory lock → capture old→new batch-tagged → mutate, every mutation through the existing lifecycle RPCs so the cycle / catch-all / typed-slug / blast-radius laws keep one authority) and `admin_undo_category_import` (reverse order, audited). New routes `src/routes/api/admin/categories/{export,import}.ts`: BOM + RFC-4180 + formula-safe CSV over the 18 ratified columns with a dated, scope-named filename, and a server-side-only parse (1 MiB / 5 000 rows, strict header plus an optional trailing `action` — upsert · create-root · retire · reactivate · delete; deletion is never inferred from absence) with a SHA-256 digest the commit must match. The attributes import dialog was generalised into `src/features/admin/import-dialog.tsx` and both consoles now render presets of it (props, never a copy — B3): `ImportAttributesDialog` (two files) and `ImportCategoriesDialog` (one file). Roster toolbar: Export categories / Import categories, both honouring the root filter as the scope. Keys EN+AM under `admin.categories.export.*` / `admin.categories.import.*`. E2E: CT-18 round-trip invariant through the file picker (0 · 0 · 0 · 0 · 0 refused, Discard leaves no capture row), CT-19 create + rename → DB truth → undo, CT-20 retire/reactivate with both audit rows, CT-21 unknown parent / cycle / catch-all parent / delete-with-children / formula / duplicate slug with nothing written, CT-22 a `categories:view`-only operator (no control, 403, 401 anonymous), CT-23 a commit without step-up (428, P0009, nothing written). Local run: 99 passed, 3 skipped (7.8m) on desktop-1280 + mobile-360.

- 2026-09-08 — IE-4a: AMHARIC CATEGORY NAMES THROUGH THE TRANSLATION DOOR. Its whole payload is re-landed text-independently: part one (mark `20260909041000`) declares `attr_import_plan` IN FULL — the post-INH-1 planner plus the IE-5 survivor rule (a pre-pass collects the links file's in-scope `unlink` pairs and the `blastRadius` verdict reads only the links that survive them) — with an in-file proof asserting both the survivor rule and the INH-1 primary-lineage walk; part two (mark `20260909042000`) declares the four-phase `admin_commit_attribute_import` and the reverse-order `admin_undo_attribute_import`, and heals the ledger by recording `20260909040000` so migration parity holds with the skipped file. The links loop now reads DIRECTNESS first (`origin` empty or equal to the row's own category slug): an inherited echo row never registers in the duplicate set and never refuses, so a direct row for the same category and key in the same file is accepted and becomes the nearest link; unlinking through an echo is still refused (`inheritedRow`). - 2026-09-09 — IMPORT-GATE (T1): ONE SECURITY GATE FOR EVERY IMPORT. Part A lands `src/server/imports/registry.ts` (one declaration per family: permission, step-up, scope kind, per-file identity header, ordered columns with a class each — identity/editable/read-only/action — identity columns, duplicate policy and caps) and `src/server/imports/gate.ts` (bearer to caller-context client, family lookup, scope shape, BOM-aware RFC-4180 parse, file identity before any row, exact header law with no duplicate or unknown column, NFC plus control/bidi/zero-width stripping, NUL and raw-formula refusal, per-column format and length law, duplicate identities, typed row payload, SHA-256 digest, one in-flight door and 30 previews a minute per operator, and an audit call per door). Part B is migration mark `20260909060000` (applied file `20260909054432`): `import_norm_text`, `import_norm_bool`, `import_norm_date`, `import_norm_pipe`, `import_is_slug`, `import_sanitize` and `import_gate_audit`, each with in-file `REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon` / `GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated` / `GRANT ALL TO service_role` and an ACL read-back. The attributes and categories import routes and both export routes now enter through the gate and own no judgement of their own; refusal vocabulary, digest and RPC contracts are unchanged (`badHeader` stays whole-file nonsense, `wrongFile` stays another family's header, `duplicateSlug` stays the category planner's verdict). Part D is `e2e/import-security.spec.ts`, parameterised over every registered family (IG-1 whole-file refusals, IG-2 per-row hostility, IG-3 digest and rate limit). Part E is `scripts/check-import-gate.sh` with its bad-route fixture, wired into CI, and `docs/features/imports.md`. Two laws the run taught the gate: identity before shape (`requireBearer` at the door, so an anonymous caller is 401 and never learns whether the body was well formed — CT-22), and option cells are the platform's own serialisation (whole-cell JSON array, or pipe segments that are each a JSON object or `value=label`), so the gate measures only their SIZE and leaves meaning — bad parent, malformed array — to the planner (AT-20, AT-25). Translations CSV/XLIFF join the gate in T2.
- 2026-09-09 — IMPORT-GATE (T2, Part C + Part D): THE UI-STRING DOOR JOINS THE GATE. `POST /api/admin/translations/import` is the only way a translation file now reaches the database: the console reads the file as text and posts it with the target language, the browser parses nothing, and `translations` is a registered family (`translations:manage`, language scope, key/value law `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]*`/200 and 4000 characters, `context` an optional trailing column). Both dialects enter the same door — XLIFF 1.2 units become the family's own columns before any refusal is spoken, and a unit with no id or no target yields an EMPTY row rather than being dropped so an operator's row numbers stay the file's own. The export now NEUTRALISES a formula-leading cell with a leading apostrophe and the gate un-neutralises on the way in, so `+ Add` survives a round trip while a raw formula in a hand-made file is refused. The family has no preview door and no digest, so the WRITE carries the one-in-flight and per-minute budget; `undo` takes the same route WITH its batch's language, so a take-back is scoped, metered and audited exactly like the run it undoes. `admin_import_translations` and `admin_undo_import` keep EXECUTE for `authenticated` and remain the sole authority on permission, step-up, unknown keys, placeholders and revision capture. `scripts/check-import-gate.sh` gained a fourth rule: an import writer called outside its route is a second door and fails the build. Part D's hostile catalogue is now parameterised over three families and gained mode-awareness (a one-step family meters a row the gate itself refuses, so the ceiling is proven with no write) plus IG-4, the XLIFF dialect. Runs: import-security 20 passed; translations console + data 41 passed; governance 14 passed with TR-19/TR-30 (`@global-state`, DEC-028) failing on catalog-page ordering unrelated to the door — INC-182.
